# Azure workload SKU catalog

**492 SKUs across 8 Azure services.** Generated 2026-08-17 by the `CurrentAzureWorkloadSKUInfo` skill, direct from Microsoft documentation.

| Service | SKUs |
| --- | --- |
| Azure SQL Database | 198 |
| Azure Database for PostgreSQL | 109 |
| Azure SQL Managed Instance | 106 |
| Azure Database for MySQL | 42 |
| Azure Virtual Machines | 20 |
| Azure App Service | 9 |
| Azure Cache for Redis | 5 |
| Azure Kubernetes Service | 3 |

Column notes:

- **Release date** — the GA date of the release that made the SKU orderable, or the preview date for SKUs still in preview. Azure announces SKUs by family or tier, so sizes inherit their family's date unless they were added later.
- **Confidence** — `high` a dated Microsoft announcement names this exact change; `medium` a Microsoft page dates it to a month; `low` reconstructed from context; **`unknown` means no date could be sourced** and the cell reads *not established* rather than carrying a guess.
- **Lifecycle status** — read from the current Microsoft documentation, including retirement dates where Microsoft has announced them.
- **When to recommend** — taken from Microsoft's own guidance columns ("Target workloads", "When to use", tier descriptions) on the pages linked in the last column.

| 1 | `Basic` | Azure SQL Database | Basic | n/a (DTU model) | 5 DTU | 2014-09 | medium | Generally available | [docs](https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/azure-sql/database/resource-limits-dtu-single-databases) | [announcement](https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/new-azure-sql-database-service-tiers-generally-available-in-september-with-reduced-pricing-and-enhanced-sla/) | 1. Purchasing model: pick DTU only if you want simple, preconfigured bundles of compute, storage, and I/O. Microsoft marks the vCore model as recommended, and Azure Hybrid Benefit, the serverless compute tier, and the Hyperscale service tier are all unavailable in the DTU model.<br>2. Use for development, testing, and other infrequently accessed workloads that are less sensitive to performance variability - Basic data files sit on HDD-based Standard page blob storage.<br>3. Only use when the database fits in 2 GB; that is the hard storage ceiling for the Basic tier.<br>4. Do not use for resource-intensive or CPU-intensive work: Basic provides less than one vCore, and Microsoft recommends S3 or greater instead.<br>5. Do not use if you need more than 7 days of point-in-time restore; Basic caps backup retention at 1-7 days while Standard and Premium go to 35 days.<br>6. Avoid if you need columnstore indexing or In-Memory OLTP - neither is available in Basic. | [1](https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/azure-sql/database/purchasing-models)<br>[2](https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/azure-sql/database/service-tiers-dtu)<br>[3](https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/azure-sql/database/resource-limits-dtu-single-databases) |
| 2 | `S0` | Azure SQL Database | Standard | n/a (DTU model) | 10 DTU | 2014-09 | medium | Generally available | [docs](https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/azure-sql/database/resource-limits-dtu-single-databases) | [announcement](https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/new-azure-sql-database-service-tiers-generally-available-in-september-with-reduced-pricing-and-enhanced-sla/) | 1. | 5 | `S3` | Azure SQL Database | Standard | n/a (DTU model) | 100 DTU | 2014-09 | medium | Generally available | [docs](https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/azure-sql/database/resource-limits-dtu-single-databases) | [announcement](https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/new-azure-sql-database-service-tiers-generally-available-in-september-with-reduced-pricing-and-enhanced-sla/) | 1. Purchasing model: pick DTU only if you want simple, preconfigured bundles of compute, storage, and I/O. Microsoft marks the vCore model as recommended, and Azure Hybrid Benefit, the serverless compute tier, and the Hyperscale service tier are all unavailable in the DTU model.<br>2. Use for development and production workloads that need up to 1 TB of storage and up to 3000 DTU of blended compute.<br>3. Choose S3 or greater whenever the workload is CPU-intensive, since S3 is the first Standard objective with a full vCore or more.<br>4. Choose S3 or greater when the database is the target of a restore operation.<br>5. S3 and above are the lowest Standard objectives that support columnstore indexing.<br>6. Move up to Premium instead if you need In-Memory OLTP or approximately 2 ms IO latency - Standard delivers about 5 ms read and 10 ms write. | [1](https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/azure-sql/database/purchasing-models)<br>[2](https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/azure-sql/database/service-tiers-dtu)<br>[3](https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/azure-sql/database/resource-limits-dtu-single-databases) |

| 11 | `P1` | Azure SQL Database | Premium | n/a (DTU model) | 125 DTU | 2014-09 | medium | Generally available | [docs](https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/azure-sql/database/resource-limits-dtu-single-databases) | [announcement](https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/new-azure-sql-database-service-tiers-generally-available-in-september-with-reduced-pricing-and-enhanced-sla/) | 1. Purchasing model: pick DTU only if you want simple, preconfigured bundles of compute, storage, and I/O. Microsoft marks the vCore model as recommended, and Azure Hybrid Benefit, the serverless compute tier, and the Hyperscale service tier are all unavailable in the DTU model.<br>2. Use for IO-intensive production workloads: Premium delivers more than 25 IOPS per DTU versus 1-4 IOPS per DTU in Basic and Standard.<br>3. Use when the workload needs low IO latency - approximately 2 ms read and write, against about 5 ms read and 10 ms write in Basic and Standard.<br>4. Choose Premium when you need In-Memory OLTP, which is not available in the Basic or Standard tiers.<br>5. Choose P11 or P15 when the database needs more than 1 TB of storage, up to the 4 TB Premium maximum.<br>6. Check region support before choosing P11 or P15 above 1 TB: storage is capped at 1 TB in China East, China North, Germany Central, and Germany Northeast. | [1](https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/azure-sql/database/purchasing-models)<br>[2](https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/azure-sql/database/service-tiers-dtu)<br>[3](https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/azure-sql/database/resource-limits-dtu-single-databases) |

| 17 | `BasicPool_50` | Azure SQL Database | Basic | n/a (DTU model) | 50 eDTU | 2014-09 | medium | Generally available | [docs](https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/azure-sql/database/resource-limits-dtu-elastic-pools) | [announcement](https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/new-azure-sql-database-service-tiers-generally-available-in-september-with-reduced-pricing-and-enhanced-sla/) | 1. Purchasing model: pick DTU only if you want simple, preconfigured bundles of compute, storage, and I/O. Microsoft marks the vCore model as recommended, and Azure Hybrid Benefit, the serverless compute tier, and the Hyperscale service tier are all unavailable in the DTU model.<br>2. Use when you have many databases with a low average resource utilization and relatively infrequent utilization spikes.<br>3. Use when per-database usage is unpredictable and you want a single, predictable budget for the group rather than provisioning each database separately.<br>4. Use when you want a guarantee that no single database can consume all the resources, while every database keeps a minimum reservation.<br>5. Move a database out of the pool and onto a single database if it shows consistently high utilization that impacts the others.<br>6. Match the pool tier to the databases: Basic pools cap at 5 eDTU and 2 GB per database, Standard pools at 3000 eDTU, Premium pools at 4000 eDTU and 100 databases per pool. | [1](https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/azure-sql/database/purchasing-models)<br>[2](https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/azure-sql/database/service-tiers-dtu)<br>[3](https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/azure-sql/database/resource-limits-dtu-elastic-pools)<br>[4](https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/azure-sql/database/elastic-pool-overview) |

Move a database out of the pool and onto a single database if it shows consistently high utilization that impacts the others.<br>6. Match the pool tier to the databases: Basic pools cap at 5 eDTU and 2 GB per database, Standard pools at 3000 eDTU, Premium pools at 4000 eDTU and 100 databases per pool. | [1](https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/azure-sql/database/purchasing-models)<br>[2](https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/azure-sql/database/service-tiers-dtu)<br>[3](https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/azure-sql/database/resource-limits-dtu-elastic-pools)<br>[4](https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/azure-sql/database/elastic-pool-overview) |

| 46 | `GP_S_Gen5_1` | Azure SQL Database | General Purpose - Serverless | Standard-series (Gen5) | 1 vCore | 2019-11 | medium | Generally available | [docs](https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/azure-sql/database/resource-limits-vcore-single-databases) | [announcement](https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/azure-sql/database/serverless-tier-overview) | 1. Purchasing model: vCore is the model Microsoft recommends. Choose it when you value flexibility, control, and transparency, want to scale compute and storage independently, or want to apply Azure Hybrid Benefit or reserved capacity pricing.<br>2. Use for single databases with intermittent, unpredictable usage interspersed with periods of inactivity and low average compute utilization over time.<br>3. Use for databases you currently rescale frequently and would rather delegate compute rescaling to the service.<br>4. Use for new databases with no usage history, where compute sizing is hard or impossible to estimate before deployment.<br>5. Only choose it if the workload can tolerate warm-up delay after an idle period, and the application implements connection retry logic - auto-resume connectivity errors are predictable.<br>6. Do not choose it for regular, predictable usage with high average utilization; provisioned compute has a lower vCore unit price for those.<br>7. Do not choose it for several intermittent databases that could instead be consolidated into an elastic pool for better price-performance.<br>8. Availability constraint: serverless runs only on standard-series (Gen5), only in the vCore model, and is not offered in Business Critical. | [1](https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/azure-sql/database/purchasing-models)<br>[2](https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/azure-sql/database/serverless-tier-overview)<br>[3](https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/azure-sql/database/service-tiers-sql-database-vcore) |

Do not choose it for regular, predictable usage with high average utilization; provisioned compute has a lower vCore unit price for those.<br>7. Do not choose it for several intermittent databases that could instead be consolidated into an elastic pool for better price-performance.<br>8. Availability constraint: serverless runs only on standard-series (Gen5), only in the vCore model, and is not offered in Business Critical. | [1](https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/azure-sql/database/purchasing-models)<br>[2](https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/azure-sql/database/serverless-tier-overview)<br>[3](https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/azure-sql/database/service-tiers-sql-database-vcore) |

Microsoft positions premium-series memory optimized as the successor to the retired M-series hardware, with more memory at a lower price.<br>5. Check regional availability first - memory optimized premium-series is offered in a subset of regions.<br>6. | 90 | `HS_PRMS_2` | Azure SQL Database | Hyperscale | Premium-series | 2 vCore / 10.4 GB | 2023-07 | high | Generally available | [docs](https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/azure-sql/database/resource-limits-vcore-single-databases) | [announcement](https://aka.ms/AAiq28n) | 1. Purchasing model: vCore is the model Microsoft recommends. Choose it when you value flexibility, control, and transparency, want to scale compute and storage independently, or want to apply Azure Hybrid Benefit or reserved capacity pricing.<br>2. Choose over standard-series when you want a guarantee of running on newer CPUs; standard-series gives no such guarantee and may be placed on older hardware.<br>3. Choose when a single Hyperscale database or pool needs more than the 80 vCores that standard-series tops out at - premium-series reaches 128.<br>4. Cost is not a reason to avoid it: there is no price difference between premium-series and standard-series at the same vCore count.<br>5. Check regional availability first - premium-series is not offered in every region, whereas standard-series is.<br>6. Also available for Hyperscale elastic pools, so use it when pooled Hyperscale databases need the same CPU guarantee. | [1](https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/azure-sql/database/purchasing-models)<br>[2](https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/azure-sql/database/service-tiers-sql-database-vcore)<br>[3](https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/azure-sql/database/resource-limits-vcore-single-databases) |

Purchasing model: vCore is the model Microsoft recommends. Choose it when you value flexibility, control, and transparency, want to scale compute and storage independently, or want to apply Azure Hybrid Benefit or reserved capacity pricing.<br>2. Microsoft's recommended and default service tier for all new and modernizing OLTP and HTAP workloads, not only large databases.<br>3. Use when the database may grow past the 4 TB ceiling of General Purpose and Business Critical - Hyperscale scales to 128 TB.<br>4. Use when you want to pay only for allocated data storage rather than a provisioned maximum, with a 10 GB minimum and no charge for log storage.<br>5. Use when you want to choose the number of high availability replicas from 0 to 4 to trade resiliency against cost.<br>6. Use when you need read scale-out through named replicas, fast scaling without waiting for data to copy, or fast restore of a large database.<br>7. Do not choose it if the workload requires In-Memory OLTP tables, which Hyperscale does not support.<br>8. Note that Azure Hybrid Benefit is not available for new Hyperscale databases as of December 2023. | [1](https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/azure-sql/database/purchasing-models)<br>[2](https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/azure-sql/database/service-tiers-sql-database-vcore)<br>[3](https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/azure-sql/database/service-tier-hyperscale) |

Use when you want to choose the number of high availability replicas from 0 to 4 to trade resiliency against cost.<br>6. Use when you need read scale-out through named replicas, fast scaling without waiting for data to copy, or fast restore of a large database.<br>7. Do not choose it if the workload requires In-Memory OLTP tables, which Hyperscale does not support.<br>8. Note that Azure Hybrid Benefit is not available for new Hyperscale databases as of December 2023. | [1](https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/azure-sql/database/purchasing-models)<br>[2](https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/azure-sql/database/service-tiers-sql-database-vcore)<br>[3](https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/azure-sql/database/service-tier-hyperscale) |

Confirm your subscription qualifies: DC-series requires a paid offer type such as Pay-As-You-Go or Enterprise Agreement.<br>5. Do not choose it if you need the serverless compute tier or zone redundancy - DC-series supports neither.<br>6. Check regional availability first; DC-series is offered only in selected regions, unlike standard-series.<br>7. Size within 2-40 vCores at 4.5 GB of memory per vCore, which is below the 5.1 GB per vCore of standard-series. | [1](https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/azure-sql/database/purchasing-models)<br>[2](https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/azure-sql/database/service-tiers-sql-database-vcore)<br>[3](https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/azure-sql/database/resource-limits-vcore-single-databases) |

| 134 | `GP_Gen5_2` | Azure SQL Database | General Purpose | Standard-series (Gen5) | 2 vCore / 10.4 GB | 2018-04 | low | Generally available | [docs](https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/azure-sql/database/resource-limits-vcore-single-databases) | [announcement](https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/a-flexible-new-way-to-purchase-azure-sql-database/) | 1. Purchasing model: vCore is the model Microsoft recommends. Choose it when you value flexibility, control, and transparency, want to scale compute and storage independently, or want to apply Azure Hybrid Benefit or reserved capacity pricing.<br>2. Default choice for most generic Azure SQL Database workloads that need a fully managed engine with the standard SLA.<br>3. Use when storage latency between 5 ms and 10 ms is acceptable for the workload.<br>4. Use for budget-oriented workloads wanting balanced compute and storage, at roughly a third the price of Business Critical for the same vCore count.<br>5. Use when the database fits within 4 TB; move to Hyperscale beyond that.<br>6. Standard-series (Gen5) is available in every public region worldwide, so pick it when broad regional coverage matters.<br>7. Do not choose it if you need In-Memory OLTP tables or a free read-scale replica - both require Business Critical. | [1](https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/azure-sql/database/purchasing-models)<br>[2](https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/azure-sql/database/service-tiers-sql-database-vcore)<br>[3](https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/azure-sql/database/resource-limits-vcore-single-databases) |

Choose it when you value flexibility, control, and transparency, want to scale compute and storage independently, or want to apply Azure Hybrid Benefit or reserved capacity pricing.<br>2. Default choice for most generic Azure SQL Database workloads that need a fully managed engine with the standard SLA.<br>3. Use when storage latency between 5 ms and 10 ms is acceptable for the workload.<br>4. Use for budget-oriented workloads wanting balanced compute and storage, at roughly a third the price of Business Critical for the same vCore count.<br>5. Use when the database fits within 4 TB; move to Hyperscale beyond that.<br>6. Standard-series (Gen5) is available in every public region worldwide, so pick it when broad regional coverage matters.<br>7. Do not choose it if you need In-Memory OLTP tables or a free read-scale replica - both require Business Critical. | [1](https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/azure-sql/database/purchasing-models)<br>[2](https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/azure-sql/database/service-tiers-sql-database-vcore)<br>[3](https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/azure-sql/database/resource-limits-vcore-single-databases) |

Purchasing model: vCore is the model Microsoft recommends. Choose it when you value flexibility, control, and transparency, want to scale compute and storage independently, or want to apply Azure Hybrid Benefit or reserved capacity pricing.<br>2. Do not select for new deployments - Fsv2-series can no longer be created and is retired on 1 October 2026.<br>3. If you are already on Fsv2-series, plan a move to Hyperscale premium-series or standard-series (Gen5), which Microsoft states give similar or better price-performance for most databases and workloads.<br>4. Validate the target hardware against your own workload before migrating, as Microsoft recommends, rather than assuming parity.<br>5. Historically chosen for CPU-intensive General Purpose workloads: Fsv2 sustains a 3.4 GHz all-core turbo and scales to 72 vCores.<br>6. Never a fit for workloads sensitive to memory or tempdb per vCore - Fsv2 gives 1.9 GB per vCore against 5.1 GB on standard-series.<br>7. Only ever available in the General Purpose service tier. | [1](https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/azure-sql/database/purchasing-models)<br>[2](https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/azure-sql/database/service-tiers-sql-database-vcore)<br>[3](https://azure.microsoft.com/updates?id=485030) |

Purchasing model: vCore is the model Microsoft recommends. Choose it when you value flexibility, control, and transparency, want to scale compute and storage independently, or want to apply Azure Hybrid Benefit or reserved capacity pricing.<br>2. Choose when you need Always Encrypted with secure enclaves backed by hardware enclaves (Intel SGX) rather than Virtualization-based Security enclaves.<br>3. Confirm your subscription qualifies: DC-series requires a paid offer type such as Pay-As-You-Go or Enterprise Agreement.<br>5. Do not choose it if you need the serverless compute tier or zone redundancy - DC-series supports neither.<br>6. Check regional availability first; DC-series is offered only in selected regions, unlike standard-series.<br>7. Size within 2-40 vCores at 4.5 GB of memory per vCore, which is below the 5.1 GB per vCore of standard-series. | [1](https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/azure-sql/database/purchasing-models)<br>[2](https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/azure-sql/database/service-tiers-sql-database-vcore)<br>[3](https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/azure-sql/database/resource-limits-vcore-single-databases) |

Choose it when you value flexibility, control, and transparency, want to scale compute and storage independently, or want to apply Azure Hybrid Benefit or reserved capacity pricing.<br>2. Choose when you need Always Encrypted with secure enclaves backed by hardware enclaves (Intel SGX) rather than Virtualization-based Security enclaves.<br>3. Confirm your subscription qualifies: DC-series requires a paid offer type such as Pay-As-You-Go or Enterprise Agreement.<br>5. Do not choose it if you need the serverless compute tier or zone redundancy - DC-series supports neither.<br>6. Check regional availability first; DC-series is offered only in selected regions, unlike standard-series.<br>7. | 172 | `BC_Gen5_2` | Azure SQL Database | Business Critical | Standard-series (Gen5) | 2 vCore / 10.4 GB | 2018-04 | low | Generally available | [docs](https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/azure-sql/database/resource-limits-vcore-single-databases) | [announcement](https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/a-flexible-new-way-to-purchase-azure-sql-database/) | 1. Purchasing model: vCore is the model Microsoft recommends. Choose it when you value flexibility, control, and transparency, want to scale compute and storage independently, or want to apply Azure Hybrid Benefit or reserved capacity pricing.<br>2. Use when the workload needs consistently low IO latency, about 1-2 ms on average, from locally attached SSD storage.<br>3. Use when reporting, analytics, or other read-only queries can be offloaded to the free read-scale replica included in the tier.<br>4. Use when you need higher resiliency and faster recovery: three secondary replicas mean a secondary is promoted immediately on failure.<br>5. Use when the workload needs In-Memory OLTP, which General Purpose and Hyperscale do not offer.<br>6. Use when active geo-replication must guarantee an RPO of 5 seconds and an RTO of 30 seconds.<br>7. Budget for roughly 2.7 times the General Purpose price, which is what the three extra replicas cost. | [1](https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/azure-sql/database/purchasing-models)<br>[2](https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/azure-sql/database/service-tiers-sql-database-vcore)<br>[3](https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/azure-sql/database/resource-limits-vcore-single-databases) |

Use when reporting, analytics, or other read-only queries can be offloaded to the free read-scale replica included in the tier.<br>4. Use when you need higher resiliency and faster recovery: three secondary replicas mean a secondary is promoted immediately on failure.<br>5. Use when the workload needs In-Memory OLTP, which General Purpose and Hyperscale do not offer.<br>6. Use when active geo-replication must guarantee an RPO of 5 seconds and an RTO of 30 seconds.<br>7. Budget for roughly 2.7 times the General Purpose price, which is what the three extra replicas cost. | [1](https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/azure-sql/database/purchasing-models)<br>[2](https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/azure-sql/database/service-tiers-sql-database-vcore)<br>[3](https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/azure-sql/database/resource-limits-vcore-single-databases) |

Default service tier for most generic managed instance workloads that need a fully managed engine with the standard SLA.<br>3. Use when storage latency of 5-10 ms is acceptable for the workload.<br>4. Use when you need SQL Server instance-scoped surface area and near-100% engine compatibility rather than a single database.<br>5. Use when you can stop and start the instance during idle periods to save compute and licensing cost - a General Purpose-only capability.<br>6. Stay within the limits: 80 vCores, 16 TB of instance storage, and 100 user databases per instance.<br>7. Do not choose it if you need In-Memory OLTP or a read-only replica; those require Business Critical.<br>8. Baseline managed instance hardware - choose it when 5.1 GB of memory per vCore and a ceiling of 80 vCores and 408 GB are enough.<br>9. Choose it when regional coverage matters: standard-series is available in all public regions worldwide.<br>10. Use an instance pool if you want a 2-vCore instance; 2 vCores cannot be deployed as a standalone instance.<br>11. Use when you need SQL Server instance-scoped surface area and near-100% engine compatibility rather than a single database.<br>5. Use when you can stop and start the instance during idle periods to save compute and licensing cost - a General Purpose-only capability.<br>6. Stay within the limits: 80 vCores, 16 TB of instance storage, and 100 user databases per instance.<br>7. Do not choose it if you need In-Memory OLTP or a read-only replica; those require Business Critical.<br>8. Baseline managed instance hardware - choose it when 5.1 GB of memory per vCore and a ceiling of 80 vCores and 408 GB are enough.<br>9. Choose it when regional coverage matters: standard-series is available in all public regions worldwide.<br>10. Use an instance pool if you want a 2-vCore instance; 2 vCores cannot be deployed as a standalone instance.<br>11. Move to premium-series if the workload needs more memory per vCore or more than 80 vCores. | [1](https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/azure-sql/database/purchasing-models)<br>[2](https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/azure-sql/managed-instance/service-tiers-managed-instance-vcore)<br>[3](https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/azure-sql/managed-instance/resource-limits) |

| 208 | `GP_Gen5 (4 vCores)` | Azure SQL Managed Instance | Next-gen General Purpose | Standard-series (Gen5) | 4 vCore / 20.4 GB | 2025-11 | high | Generally available | [docs](https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/azure-sql/managed-instance/resource-limits) | [announcement](https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/azure-sql/managed-instance/doc-changes-updates-release-notes-whats-new-archive) | 1. Purchasing model: vCore is the model Microsoft recommends. Choose it when you value flexibility, control, and transparency, want to scale compute and storage independently, or want to apply Azure Hybrid Benefit or reserved capacity pricing.<br>2. Choose when your business is budget-oriented but the performance metrics and limits of the classic General Purpose tier are insufficient - the baseline cost is the same.<br>3. Choose when you need more than 100 databases on one instance; Next-gen supports up to 500.<br>4. Choose when you need more than 16 TB of reserved storage; Next-gen supports up to 32 TB.<br>5. Choose when you need better storage latency, IOPS, and throughput - it uses Elastic SAN instead of page blobs, giving 3-5 ms latency against 5-10 ms.<br>6. Choose when you want to scale vCores, memory, storage, and IOPS independently, including flexible memory on premium-series hardware.<br>7. Budget for the extras: IOPS above the free quota of 3 per GB of reserved storage, and memory above the default allocation, are billed separately.<br>8. Note that In-Memory OLTP is still not supported, and there are still no read-only replicas.<br>9. Baseline managed instance hardware - choose it when 5.1 GB of memory per vCore and a ceiling of 80 vCores and 408 GB are enough.<br>10. Choose it when regional coverage matters: standard-series is available in all public regions worldwide.<br>11. Use an instance pool if you want a 2-vCore instance; 2 vCores cannot be deployed as a standalone instance.<br>12. Move to premium-series if the workload needs more memory per vCore or more than 80 vCores. | [1](https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/azure-sql/database/purchasing-models)<br>[2](https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/azure-sql/managed-instance/service-tiers-managed-instance-vcore)<br>[3](https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/azure-sql/managed-instance/service-tiers-next-gen-general-purpose-use)<br>[4](https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/azure-sql/managed-instance/resource-limits) |

Use when you need In-Memory OLTP, which neither General Purpose nor Next-gen General Purpose supports.<br>6. Use when advanced data corruption protection through automatic page repair matters.<br>7. Use when a failover group must guarantee an RPO of 5 seconds and an RTO of 30 seconds.<br>8. Configure 64 or more vCores if you need compute isolation - it is only supported at that size and above.<br>9. Plan capacity accordingly: one Business Critical vCore consumes four regional vCore units against your subscription quota, versus one for General Purpose.<br>10. Baseline managed instance hardware - choose it when 5.1 GB of memory per vCore and a ceiling of 80 vCores and 408 GB are enough.<br>11. Choose it when regional coverage matters: standard-series is available in all public regions worldwide.<br>12. Choose when the workload needs 7 GB of memory per vCore instead of 5.1 GB, up to 560 GB per instance.<br>10. Choose when you need more than 80 vCores - premium-series scales to 128 in Business Critical and Next-gen General Purpose.<br>11. Required if you want the flexible memory feature, which lets you raise memory without adding vCores.<br>12. Choose when Business Critical needs up to 16 TB of storage, available in major regions (5.5 TB elsewhere).<br>13. | 233 | `GP_G8IM (4 vCores)` | Azure SQL Managed Instance | Next-gen General Purpose | Premium-series | 4 vCore / 28 GB | 2025-11 | high | Generally available | [docs](https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/azure-sql/managed-instance/resource-limits) | [announcement](https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/azure-sql/managed-instance/doc-changes-updates-release-notes-whats-new-archive) | 1. Purchasing model: vCore is the model Microsoft recommends. Choose it when you value flexibility, control, and transparency, want to scale compute and storage independently, or want to apply Azure Hybrid Benefit or reserved capacity pricing.<br>2. Choose when your business is budget-oriented but the performance metrics and limits of the classic General Purpose tier are insufficient - the baseline cost is the same.<br>3. Choose when you need more than 100 databases on one instance; Next-gen supports up to 500.<br>4. Choose when you need more than 16 TB of reserved storage; Next-gen supports up to 32 TB.<br>5. Choose when you need better storage latency, IOPS, and throughput - it uses Elastic SAN instead of page blobs, giving 3-5 ms latency against 5-10 ms.<br>6. Choose when you want to scale vCores, memory, storage, and IOPS independently, including flexible memory on premium-series hardware.<br>7. Budget for the extras: IOPS above the free quota of 3 per GB of reserved storage, and memory above the default allocation, are billed separately.<br>8. Note that In-Memory OLTP is still not supported, and there are still no read-only replicas.<br>9. Choose when you want the newer Intel 8370C (Ice Lake) 2.8 GHz processors rather than the mixed Broadwell/Skylake/Cascade Lake pool of standard-series.<br>10. Choose when the workload needs 7 GB of memory per vCore instead of 5.1 GB, up to 560 GB per instance.<br>11. Choose when you need more than 80 vCores - premium-series scales to 128 in Business Critical and Next-gen General Purpose.<br>12. Required if you want the flexible memory feature, which lets you raise memory without adding vCores.<br>13. Choose when Business Critical needs up to 16 TB of storage, available in major regions (5.5 TB elsewhere).<br>14. Regional coverage is broad: premium-series is available in all public regions. | [1](https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/azure-sql/database/purchasing-models)<br>[2](https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/azure-sql/managed-instance/service-tiers-managed-instance-vcore)<br>[3](https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/azure-sql/managed-instance/service-tiers-next-gen-general-purpose-use)<br>[4](https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/azure-sql/managed-instance/resource-limits) |

Purchasing model: vCore is the model Microsoft recommends. Choose it when you value flexibility, control, and transparency, want to scale compute and storage independently, or want to apply Azure Hybrid Benefit or reserved capacity pricing.<br>2. Use when the workload needs consistently low IO latency, about 1-2 ms, from locally attached SSD storage.<br>3. Use when reports, analytics, or read-only queries can be redirected to the free read-scale replica included in the tier.<br>4. Use when you need higher resiliency and faster recovery - one of three secondary replicas becomes the new primary immediately on failure.<br>5. Use when you need In-Memory OLTP, which neither General Purpose nor Next-gen General Purpose supports.<br>6. Use when advanced data corruption protection through automatic page repair matters.<br>7. Use when a failover group must guarantee an RPO of 5 seconds and an RTO of 30 seconds.<br>8. Configure 64 or more vCores if you need compute isolation - it is only supported at that size and above.<br>9. Plan capacity accordingly: one Business Critical vCore consumes four regional vCore units against your subscription quota, versus one for General Purpose.<br>10. Choose when you want the newer Intel 8370C (Ice Lake) 2.8 GHz processors rather than the mixed Broadwell/Skylake/Cascade Lake pool of standard-series.<br>11. Choose when the workload needs 7 GB of memory per vCore instead of 5.1 GB, up to 560 GB per instance.<br>12. Choose when you need more than 80 vCores - premium-series scales to 128 in Business Critical and Next-gen General Purpose.<br>13. Required if you want the flexible memory feature, which lets you raise memory without adding vCores.<br>14. Choose when Business Critical needs up to 16 TB of storage, available in major regions (5.5 TB elsewhere).<br>15. Purchasing model: vCore is the model Microsoft recommends. Choose it when you value flexibility, control, and transparency, want to scale compute and storage independently, or want to apply Azure Hybrid Benefit or reserved capacity pricing.<br>2. Use when the workload needs consistently low IO latency, about 1-2 ms, from locally attached SSD storage.<br>3. Use when reports, analytics, or read-only queries can be redirected to the free read-scale replica included in the tier.<br>4. Use when you need higher resiliency and faster recovery - one of three secondary replicas becomes the new primary immediately on failure.<br>5. Use when you need In-Memory OLTP, which neither General Purpose nor Next-gen General Purpose supports.<br>6. Use when advanced data corruption protection through automatic page repair matters.<br>7. Use when a failover group must guarantee an RPO of 5 seconds and an RTO of 30 seconds.<br>8. Configure 64 or more vCores if you need compute isolation - it is only supported at that size and above.<br>9. Plan capacity accordingly: one Business Critical vCore consumes four regional vCore units against your subscription quota, versus one for General Purpose.<br>10. Choose when you want the newer Intel 8370C (Ice Lake) 2.8 GHz processors rather than the mixed Broadwell/Skylake/Cascade Lake pool of standard-series.<br>11. Choose when the workload needs 7 GB of memory per vCore instead of 5.1 GB, up to 560 GB per instance.<br>12. Choose when you need more than 80 vCores - premium-series scales to 128 in Business Critical and Next-gen General Purpose.<br>13. Required if you want the flexible memory feature, which lets you raise memory without adding vCores.<br>14. Choose when Business Critical needs up to 16 TB of storage, available in major regions (5.5 TB elsewhere).<br>15. Regional coverage is broad: premium-series is available in all public regions. | [1](https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/azure-sql/database/purchasing-models)<br>[2](https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/azure-sql/managed-instance/service-tiers-managed-instance-vcore)<br>[3](https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/azure-sql/managed-instance/resource-limits) |

Use when you need higher resiliency and faster recovery - one of three secondary replicas becomes the new primary immediately on failure.<br>5. Use when you need In-Memory OLTP, which neither General Purpose nor Next-gen General Purpose supports.<br>6. Use when advanced data corruption protection through automatic page repair matters.<br>7. Choose when the workload needs 7 GB of memory per vCore instead of 5.1 GB, up to 560 GB per instance.<br>12. Choose when you need more than 80 vCores - premium-series scales to 128 in Business Critical and Next-gen General Purpose.<br>13. Required if you want the flexible memory feature, which lets you raise memory without adding vCores.<br>14. Purchasing model: vCore is the model Microsoft recommends. Choose it when you value flexibility, control, and transparency, want to scale compute and storage independently, or want to apply Azure Hybrid Benefit or reserved capacity pricing.<br>2. Default service tier for most generic managed instance workloads that need a fully managed engine with the standard SLA.<br>3. Use when storage latency of 5-10 ms is acceptable for the workload.<br>4. Use when you need SQL Server instance-scoped surface area and near-100% engine compatibility rather than a single database.<br>5. Use when you can stop and start the instance during idle periods to save compute and licensing cost - a General Purpose-only capability.<br>6. Stay within the limits: 80 vCores, 16 TB of instance storage, and 100 user databases per instance.<br>7. Do not choose it if you need In-Memory OLTP or a read-only replica; those require Business Critical.<br>8. Choose when the workload is memory-bound and needs 13.6 GB of memory per vCore, up to 870.4 GB per instance.<br>9. Choose when In-Memory OLTP needs the largest available allocation - memory optimized premium-series gives roughly double the premium-series limit.<br>10. Choose when Business Critical needs up to 16 TB of instance storage.<br>11. Check regional availability first - memory optimized premium-series is restricted to a subset of regions, unlike standard-series and premium-series.<br>12. Note the memory-to-vCore ratio holds only up to 64 vCores; above that, memory stays capped at 870.4 GB. | [1](https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/azure-sql/database/purchasing-models)<br>[2](https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/azure-sql/managed-instance/service-tiers-managed-instance-vcore)<br>[3](https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/azure-sql/managed-instance/resource-limits) |

Choose when the workload is memory-bound and needs 13.6 GB of memory per vCore, up to 870.4 GB per instance.<br>10. Note that In-Memory OLTP is still not supported, and there are still no read-only replicas.<br>9. Choose when the workload is memory-bound and needs 13.6 GB of memory per vCore, up to 870.4 GB per instance.<br>10. Choose when In-Memory OLTP needs the largest available allocation - memory optimized premium-series gives roughly double the premium-series limit.<br>11. Choose when Business Critical needs up to 16 TB of instance storage.<br>12. Check regional availability first - memory optimized premium-series is restricted to a subset of regions, unlike standard-series and premium-series.<br>13. Note the memory-to-vCore ratio holds only up to 64 vCores; above that, memory stays capped at 870.4 GB. | [1](https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/azure-sql/database/purchasing-models)<br>[2](https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/azure-sql/managed-instance/service-tiers-managed-instance-vcore)<br>[3](https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/azure-sql/managed-instance/service-tiers-next-gen-general-purpose-use)<br>[4](https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/azure-sql/managed-instance/resource-limits) |

| 305 | `Standard_B1ms` | Azure Database for PostgreSQL | Burstable | B-series | 1 vCore / 2 GB | 2021-11 | medium | Generally available | [docs](https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/postgresql/compute-storage/concepts-compute) | [announcement](https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/postgresql/compute-storage/concepts-compute) | 1. Designed for workloads that don't require full CPU performance continuously.<br>2. Uses a CPU credit model: credits accumulate when usage is below baseline and are consumed when usage exceeds it.<br>3. When credits are exhausted, the VM is restricted to baseline CPU, which under sustained load can cause severe performance degradation, connection timeouts, and delays or transient failures in management operations until credits rebuild.<br>4. Best suited for web servers, proof-of-concept environments, small databases, and development builds.<br>5. Not recommended for production workloads.<br>6. Confirm the SKU is offered in your target region: PostgreSQL compute generations are not available everywhere. | [1](https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/postgresql/compute-storage/concepts-compute)<br>[2](https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/postgresql/flexible-server/overview) |

Best suited for development environments, small databases, and proof-of-concept scenarios.<br>5. Not recommended for production workloads.<br>6. ## Release dates that could not be sourced

These ship as *not established* rather than as a guess. Each needs a dated Microsoft announcement before it can carry a date.

- AKS Free and Standard pricing tiers
- AKS Premium pricing tier (with Long Term Support)
- App Service IsolatedV2 plan (App Service Environment v3)
- App Service PremiumV2 plan
- App Service PremiumV3 plan
- App Service shared and dedicated tiers (Free, Shared, Basic, Standard, Premium)
- Azure Cache for Redis Basic / Standard / Premium tiers
- Azure Virtual Machine size family

## Corrections applied to the source documentation

Typographical errors found in Microsoft's published tables. Each is corrected explicitly rather than repeated or silently dropped.

| Workload | Published | Corrected to | Why | Source |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| postgresql | `D16ds_v5 / D16ds_v5` | `D16ds_v5 / D16ads_v5` | the AMD half of the pair repeats the Intel name | [link](https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/postgresql/compute-storage/concepts-compute) |
| postgresql | `E16ds_v5 / E16ds_v5` | `E16ds_v5 / E16ads_v5` | the AMD half of the pair repeats the Intel name | [link](https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/postgresql/compute-storage/concepts-compute) |
| postgresql | `E32ds_v5 / D32ads_v5` | `E32ds_v5 / E32ads_v5` | a D-series name in a Memory Optimized row | [link](https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/postgresql/compute-storage/concepts-compute) |
| postgresql | `E64ds_v5 / E64ads_v4` | `E64ds_v5 / E64ads_v5` | the AMD half is labelled v4 in a v5 row | [link](https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/postgresql/compute-storage/concepts-compute) |
